## Codebase Exploration Report: Safecast Map Radiation Data Rendering

### 1. Map Library & Rendering System

**Current Setup:**
- **Frontend Map Library:** Leaflet.js (not Mapbox GL or MapLibre)
- **Marker Clustering:** leaflet.markercluster v1.5.3 (client-side clustering)
- **File:** `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/public_html/map.html` (line 10-15 for Leaflet includes)
- **Key JavaScript:** `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/public_html/map.html` and `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/public_html/js/marker-worker.js`

### 2. Current Tile/Grid Generation Approach

**Grid-Based Clustering (Web Mercator):**
- **Implementation:** `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/main.go` lines 1277-1378 (`fastMergeMarkersByZoom` function)
- **Mechanism:** Uses pixel-based grid cells at each zoom level, NOT H3 or vector tiles
- **Key Functions:**
  - `latLonToPixel()` (line ~1235): Converts lat/lon to Web Mercator pixels
  - `fastMergeMarkersByZoom()` (line 1280-1378): Clusters markers into grid cells with O(N) complexity
  - `radiusForZoom()` (line ~1750): Calculates marker radius dynamically based on zoom level
  - `clusterMarkersForZoom()` (line 1753-1762): Applies on-the-fly clustering

**Clustering Strategy:**
- Raw markers stored at zoom=0 in database
- Clustering happens on-the-fly during query time (client requests `/stream_markers`)
- LRU tile cache (1000 entries) to avoid recomputing clusters
- Aggregation includes averaging: lat, lon, dose rate, count rate, speed, altitude, temperature, humidity

### 3. H3 / Hex Grid References

**Finding:** NO H3 or hex grid library is currently referenced anywhere in the codebase
- **go.mod:** `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/go.mod` - No `github.com/uber/h3-go` or similar H3 library
- **grep results:** Searching for "h3", "H3", "hex", "uber" only returned false positives in dependencies (no actual use)
- **DuckDB Analytics:** `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/duckdb_analytics.go` line 39-45 loads only `ducklake` and `postgres` extensions

### 4. Main Data Flow: Database → API → Map Frontend

**Database Layer:**
- **Streaming API:** `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/pkg/database/stream.go` (lines 10-79)
- **Key Endpoints:**
  - PostgreSQL queries use PostGIS spatial index: `ST_Intersects()`, `ST_MakeEnvelope()`, `ST_DWithin()` (lines 22-35)
  - SQLite/DuckDB: Simple BETWEEN queries on lat/lon (lines 38-47)
  - IndexForZoom with bounds: `idx_markers_zoom_bounds ON markers (zoom, lat, lon)` (database.go line 778, 825)

**API Streaming Layer:**
- **Endpoint:** `/stream_markers` (GET request)
- **Handler:** `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/main.go` lines 8886-9034
- **Query Parameters:** `zoom`, `minLat`, `minLon`, `maxLat`, `maxLon`, `trackID`, `trackIDs`, `show`, `format`
- **Output Formats:** JSON (Server-Sent Events) or MessagePack binary
- **Density Sampling:** Reduces markers at low zoom (zoom<8) with configurable sample rates (15% at z7, 20% at z6, 2% at z5)

**Aggregation Pipeline:**
- **Function:** `aggregateMarkers()` (line 8684-8740)
- **Strategy:** 
  - Streams markers through context
  - Uses grid key: `fmt.Sprintf("%d:%d", int(m.Lat*scale), int(m.Lon*scale))` where `scale = 2^zoom`
  - Keeps 1 marker per cell (highest dose rate, prioritizes spectrum data)
  - Emits via channel (streaming)

**Frontend Data Processing:**
- **Web Worker:** `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/public_html/js/marker-worker.js`
- **Parsing:** JSON or MessagePack decoding off main thread
- **Marker Validation:** Requires `lat` (number) and `lon` (number) fields (lines 35-37)
- **Clustering:** Client-side via `L.markerCluster` (Leaflet plugin)

### 5. Radiation Data Structure

**Marker Model:** `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/pkg/database/models.go` lines 4-30

```go
type Marker struct {
    ID          int64
    DoseRate    float64  // µSv/h (microsieverts per hour)
    Date        int64    // UNIX timestamp
    Lon         float64  // Longitude
    Lat         float64  // Latitude
    CountRate   float64  // CPS (counts per second)
    Zoom        int      // Zoom level
    Speed       float64  // Device movement speed
    TrackID     string   // Track identifier
    Altitude    float64  // Optional: elevation in metres
    Detector    string   // Optional: detector model
    Radiation   string   // Optional: radiation channels (alpha, beta, gamma)
    Temperature float64  // Optional: Celsius
    Humidity    float64  // Optional: relative humidity %
    DeviceID    string   // Optional: realtime device ID
    DeviceName  string   // Optional: human-readable device name
    Transport   string   // Optional: walk/car/bike
    Tube        string   // Optional: detector tube description
    Country     string   // Optional: country hint
    HasSpectrum bool     // Indicates gamma spectroscopy data available
}
```

### 6. DuckDB / MotherDuck Extensions

**DuckDB Initialization:** `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/duckdb_analytics.go` lines 38-46

**Currently Installed Extensions:**
- `ducklake` (DuckLake catalog for analytics tables)
- `postgres` (PostgreSQL attachment for cross-database queries)

**H3 Extension Status:**
- **NOT explicitly loaded in code**, but according to MCP server instructions, H3 is listed as a **pre-installed extension** in MotherDuck
- Available extensions: `azure`, `delta`, `ducklake`, `encodings`, `excel`, `httpdb`, `iceberg`, `icu`, `json`, `parquet`, `spatial`, **`h3`**
- H3 can be installed/loaded with: `INSTALL h3; LOAD h3;`

### 7. REST API Endpoints for Radiation Data

**Key Endpoints:** `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/rest.go` lines 48-67

- `GET /api/radiation` - Find measurements near point (lat/lon + radius)
- `GET /api/area` - Find measurements in bounding box
- `GET /api/tracks` - List radiation measurement tracks
- `GET /api/track/{id}` - Get single track data
- `GET /api/device/{id}/history` - Device history
- `GET /api/sensors` - Real-time sensor metadata
- `GET /api/sensor/{id}/current` - Latest real-time reading

**Implementation Files:**
- `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/rest_radiation.go` (lines 8-74)
- `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/rest_area.go` (lines 8-89)

### 8. Key Database Tables & Indexes

**markers table:**
- Primary columns: `id`, `doseRate`, `date`, `lon`, `lat`, `countRate`, `zoom`, `speed`, `trackID`
- Optional columns: `altitude`, `detector`, `radiation`, `temperature`, `humidity`, `has_spectrum`
- Spatial column (PostgreSQL only): `geom` (PostGIS geometry)

**indexes:**
- `idx_markers_zoom_bounds` on `(zoom, lat, lon)` - PRIMARY for rendering
- `idx_markers_trackid_zoom_bounds` on `(trackID, zoom, lat, lon)`
- `idx_markers_zoom_bounds_speed` on `(zoom, lat, lon, speed)`
- `idx_markers_identity_probe` on `(lat, lon, date, doseRate)` - For duplicate detection

### 9. Tile Cache Implementation

**LRU Cache:** `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/main.go` lines 130-133
- **Size:** 1000 cached tile entries
- **Key Generation:** `generateTileCacheKey()` (line 8502-8512)
- **Cache Format:** Stores pre-clustered `[]database.Marker` slices
- **Invalidation:** Clears on data import/deletion to ensure freshness

### 10. Performance Optimizations

- **Density-based sampling:** Reduces payload at low zoom levels (lines 8744-8771)
- **MessagePack binary format:** 60% smaller payloads vs JSON (optional, `/stream_markers?format=msgpack`)
- **Server-Sent Events (SSE):** Streaming responses avoid loading entire dataset into memory
- **Web Worker parsing:** Offloads JSON/MessagePack parsing from main thread
- **PostGIS spatial indexes:** Fast bounding box and radius queries on PostgreSQL

### Summary Table

| Aspect | Current Implementation | H3 Status |
|--------|----------------------|-----------|
| Map Library | Leaflet.js | N/A |
| Clustering | Web Mercator grid (pixel-based) | **Not used** |
| Tile Generation | On-the-fly at query time | No vector tiles |
| H3 Library | N/A | **No Go dependency** |
| DuckDB H3 Extension | Not loaded | **Available but unused** |
| Grid Structure | 2D Web Mercator pixel grid | **Not hexagonal** |
| Cache | LRU (1000 entries) | For clustered results |
| Streaming | SSE or MessagePack | Real-time capable |

Here's my assessment:

## H3 Grid Implementation Difficulty: Medium (2–4 days)

### Current setup summary
- **Clustering:** Web Mercator pixel-grid in `fastMergeMarkersByZoom()` ([main.go:1277](cmd/unified-server/main.go#L1277)) — uses `lat*2^zoom` as grid keys
- **Frontend:** Leaflet.js with `markercluster` plugin — renders point markers, not polygons
- **Streaming:** SSE or MessagePack via `/stream_markers` — streams individual marker structs
- **DuckDB:** `h3` extension is pre-installed but not loaded

---

### What's easy
- **DuckDB analytics queries:** Just `LOAD h3;` then `h3_latlng_to_cell(lat, lon, 7)` — works immediately for analytics/MCP tools
- **Server-side aggregation key:** Replace the current `fmt.Sprintf("%d:%d", int(lat*scale), int(lon*scale))` grid key with an H3 cell index — add `github.com/uber/h3-go/v4`, map zoom→H3 resolution (e.g., zoom 5→res 4, zoom 8→res 6, zoom 12→res 9)

### What's medium
- **New API response format:** Instead of streaming individual `Marker` structs, return `{h3_cell, avg_dose, count, max_dose}` — needs a new endpoint or format flag on `/stream_markers`
- **H3 resolution↔zoom mapping:** Needs tuning so hex density feels right at each zoom level

### What's hard
- **Leaflet hexagon rendering:** Leaflet has no native H3 support. Options:
  - **`h3-js` + GeoJSON polygons** — moderate JS complexity, fine for <10k cells
  - **Switch to MapLibre GL** — better performance, significant frontend rewrite
  - **Canvas overlay** — custom but performant

The current SSE streaming model is designed for point markers. H3 mode needs to aggregate all cells first, *then* respond — which breaks the streaming approach unless you stream cell-by-cell (still feasible).

---

### Recommended approach (minimal disruption)

1. Add `github.com/uber/h3-go/v4` to go.mod
2. Add `?format=h3&resolution=auto` option to `/stream_markers` that returns H3 cells instead of points
3. Load `h3` extension in [duckdb_analytics.go](cmd/unified-server/duckdb_analytics.go) for analytics queries
4. On the frontend, add `h3-js` and a toggle that switches the map layer from point markers → GeoJSON hexagons colored by dose rate

This keeps the existing point-marker system intact while adding H3 as an optional layer — lower risk, easier to test.

---

**Biggest unknown:** Whether you want H3 to *replace* the current clustering entirely, or be a new optional overlay. Replacement is 2x the work. Overlay is safer.

8680	// aggregateMarkers chooses the most radioactive marker per grid cell while merging the
8681	// static query feed with an optional live stream. Keeping the grid map inside the goroutine
8682	// lets us reuse previous emissions and drop later duplicates without mutexes.
8683	// Markers with spectral data are prioritized over those without.
8684	func aggregateMarkers(ctx context.Context, base <-chan database.Marker, updates <-chan database.Marker, zoom int) <-chan database.Marker {
8685		out := make(chan database.Marker)
8686		go func() {
8687			defer close(out)
8688			cells := make(map[string]database.Marker)
8689			scale := math.Pow(2, float64(zoom))
8690			baseCh := base
8691			updateCh := updates
8692	
8693			emit := func(m database.Marker) {
8694				key := fmt.Sprintf("%d:%d", int(m.Lat*scale), int(m.Lon*scale))
8695				prev, exists := cells[key]
8696	
8697				// Decide if this marker should replace the previous one:
8698				// 1. No previous marker exists, OR
8699				// 2. New marker has spectrum and previous doesn't, OR
8700				// 3. Both have same spectrum status and new has higher dose rate
8701				shouldReplace := !exists ||
8702					(m.HasSpectrum && !prev.HasSpectrum) ||
8703					(m.HasSpectrum == prev.HasSpectrum && m.DoseRate > prev.DoseRate)
8704	
8705				if shouldReplace {
8706					cells[key] = m
8707					select {
8708					case out <- m:
8709					case <-ctx.Done():
8710					}
8711				}
8712			}
8713	
8714			for {
8715				select {
8716				case <-ctx.Done():
8717					return
8718				case m, ok := <-baseCh:
8719					if !ok {
8720						baseCh = nil
8721						if baseCh == nil && updateCh == nil {
8722							return
8723						}
8724						continue
8725					}
8726					emit(m)
8727				case m, ok := <-updateCh:
8728					if !ok {
8729						updateCh = nil
8730						if baseCh == nil && updateCh == nil {
8731							return
8732						}
8733						continue
8734					}
8735					emit(m)
8736				}
8737			}
8738		}()
8739		return out
8740	}
8741	
8742	// calculateSampleRate determines what percentage of markers to send based on zoom level.
8743	// Lower zoom = higher density = lower sample rate to reduce network traffic and client load.
8744	func calculateSampleRate(zoom int, minLat, minLon, maxLat, maxLon float64) float64 {
8745		// Always send all markers for track view or high zoom levels
8746		if zoom >= 8 {
8747			return 1.0
8748		}
8749	
8750		// Calculate viewport area (approximate)
8751		latSpan := maxLat - minLat
8752		lonSpan := maxLon - minLon
8753		area := latSpan * lonSpan
8754	
8755		// For very large areas at low zoom, more aggressive sampling is needed
8756		// At zoom 7, Europe view is ~9.5 lat × 19.5 lon ≈ 185 sq degrees
8757		_ = area // Used for future density calculations
8758	
8759		// Sample rate based on zoom level to achieve target performance

8973		// Helper to write a msgpack frame: 4-byte length prefix + data
8974		writeMsgpackFrame := func(data []byte) {
8975			lenBuf := make([]byte, 4)
8976			binary.BigEndian.PutUint32(lenBuf, uint32(len(data)))
8977			w.Write(lenBuf)
8978			w.Write(data)

8983			if useMsgpack {

8986			} else {

8989			}
8990		}

8992

8994			select {
8995			case <-ctx.Done():
8996				return
8997			case err, ok := <-errCh:
8998				if !ok {
8999					errCh = nil
9000					continue
9001				}
9002				if err != nil {
9003					if useMsgpack {
9004						// Send zero-length frame as error indicator
9005						w.Write([]byte{0, 0, 0, 0})
9006					} else {
9007						fmt.Fprintf(w, "event: done\ndata: %v\n\n", err)
9008					}
9009					flusher.Flush()
9010					return
9011				}
9012				errCh = nil
9013			case m, ok := <-agg:
9014				if !ok {
9015					if useMsgpack {
9016						// Send zero-length frame as end marker
9017						w.Write([]byte{0, 0, 0, 0})
9018					} else {
9019						fmt.Fprint(w, "event: done\ndata: end\n\n")
9020					}
9021					flusher.Flush()
9022					return
9023				}
9024				if useMsgpack {

1	package main
2	
3	import (
4		"net/http"
5		"strconv"
6	)
7	
8	// handleRadiation handles GET /api/radiation
9	//
10	// @Summary     Find radiation measurements near a location
11	// @Description Returns historical bGeigie measurements within a radius of the given coordinates, sorted by most recent. Uses PostGIS spatial index for fast queries. Falls back to Safecast REST API if no database is configured.
12	// @Tags        historical
13	// @Produce     json
14	// @Param       lat      query  number  true  "Latitude in decimal degrees (-90 to 90)"
15	// @Param       lon      query  number  true  "Longitude in decimal degrees (-180 to 180)"
16	// @Param       radius_m query  number  false "Search radius in meters (25 to 50000)" default(1500)
17	// @Param       limit    query  integer false "Maximum number of results (1 to 10000)" default(25)
18	// @Success     200 {object} map[string]interface{} "Radiation measurements with count, source, and query metadata"
19	// @Failure     400 {object} map[string]string "Invalid parameters"
20	// @Router      /radiation [get]
21	func (h *RESTHandler) handleRadiation(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
22		if r.Method != http.MethodGet {
23			writeError(w, http.StatusMethodNotAllowed, "method not allowed")
24			return
25		}
26		q := r.URL.Query()
27	
28		latStr := q.Get("lat")
29		lonStr := q.Get("lon")
30		if latStr == "" || lonStr == "" {
31			writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "lat and lon are required")
32			return
33		}
34		lat, err := strconv.ParseFloat(latStr, 64)
35		if err != nil || lat < -90 || lat > 90 {
36			writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "lat must be a number between -90 and 90")
37			return
38		}
39		lon, err := strconv.ParseFloat(lonStr, 64)
40		if err != nil || lon < -180 || lon > 180 {
41			writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "lon must be a number between -180 and 180")
42			return
43		}
44	
45		radiusM := 1500.0
46		if s := q.Get("radius_m"); s != "" {
47			radiusM, err = strconv.ParseFloat(s, 64)
48			if err != nil || radiusM < 25 || radiusM > 50000 {
49				writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "radius_m must be between 25 and 50000")
50				return
51			}
52		}
53	
54		limit := 5
55		if s := q.Get("limit"); s != "" {
56			limit, err = strconv.Atoi(s)
57			if err != nil || limit < 1 || limit > 10000 {
58				writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "limit must be between 1 and 10000")
59				return
60			}
61		}
62		// Hard cap for API consumers (e.g. Custom GPT) that can't handle large responses
63		if limit > 10 {
64			limit = 10
65		}
66	
67		if dbAvailable() {
68			result, err := queryRadiationDB(r.Context(), lat, lon, radiusM, limit)
69			serveMCPResult(w, result, err)
70		} else {
71			result, err := queryRadiationAPI(r.Context(), lat, lon, radiusM, limit)
72			serveMCPResult(w, result, err)
73		}
74	}
75

1	package database
2	
3	import (
4		"context"
5		"fmt"
6	)
7	
8	// StreamMarkersByZoomAndBounds streams markers row by row through a channel.
9	// It avoids loading large result sets into memory and stops when the context is done.
10	func (db *Database) StreamMarkersByZoomAndBounds(ctx context.Context, zoom int, minLat, minLon, maxLat, maxLon float64, dbType string) (<-chan Marker, <-chan error) {
11		out := make(chan Marker)
12		errCh := make(chan error, 1)
13	
14		go func() {
15			defer close(out)
16			defer close(errCh)
17	
18			var query string
19			switch dbType {
20			case "pgx":
21				// Use PostGIS spatial index with ST_Intersects and && bounding box operator
22				// for optimal performance. The && operator uses the GIST index efficiently.
23				// Note: PostgreSQL folds unquoted identifiers to lowercase, so use lowercase column names
24				query = `
25	                SELECT id, doserate, date, lon, lat, countrate, zoom, COALESCE(speed, 0) as speed, trackid,
26	                       COALESCE(altitude, 0) as altitude,
27	                       COALESCE(detector, '') as detector,
28	                       COALESCE(radiation, '') as radiation,
29	                       COALESCE(temperature, 0) as temperature,
30	                       COALESCE(humidity, 0) as humidity,
31	                       COALESCE(has_spectrum, FALSE) as has_spectrum
32	                FROM markers
33	                WHERE zoom = $1
34	                  AND geom && ST_MakeEnvelope($4, $2, $5, $3, 4326)
35	                  AND ST_Intersects(geom, ST_MakeEnvelope($4, $2, $5, $3, 4326));
36	            `
37			default:
38				query = `
39	                SELECT id, doseRate, date, lon, lat, countRate, zoom, COALESCE(speed, 0) as speed, trackID,
40	                       COALESCE(altitude, 0) as altitude,
41	                       COALESCE(detector, '') as detector,
42	                       COALESCE(radiation, '') as radiation,
43	                       COALESCE(temperature, 0) as temperature,
44	                       COALESCE(humidity, 0) as humidity,
45	                       COALESCE(has_spectrum, 0) as has_spectrum
46	                FROM markers
47	                WHERE zoom = ? AND lat BETWEEN ? AND ? AND lon BETWEEN ? AND ?;
48	            `
49			}
50	
51			rows, err := db.DB.QueryContext(ctx, query, zoom, minLat, maxLat, minLon, maxLon)
52			if err != nil {
53				errCh <- fmt.Errorf("query markers: %w", err)
54				return
55			}
56			defer rows.Close()
57	
58			for rows.Next() {
59				var m Marker
60				if err := rows.Scan(&m.ID, &m.DoseRate, &m.Date, &m.Lon, &m.Lat, &m.CountRate, &m.Zoom, &m.Speed, &m.TrackID,
61					&m.Altitude, &m.Detector, &m.Radiation, &m.Temperature, &m.Humidity, &m.HasSpectrum); err != nil {
62					errCh <- fmt.Errorf("scan marker: %w", err)
63					return
64				}
65				select {
66				case out <- m:
67				case <-ctx.Done():
68					errCh <- ctx.Err()
69					return
70				}
71			}
72	
73			if err := rows.Err(); err != nil {
74				errCh <- fmt.Errorf("iterate markers: %w", err)
75			}
76		}()
77	
78		return out, errCh
79	}
80	
81	// StreamMarkersByTrackIDZoomAndBounds streams markers of one track within bounds.
82	// This keeps memory usage low while focusing on a single track only.
83	func (db *Database) StreamMarkersByTrackIDZoomAndBounds(ctx context.Context, trackID string, zoom int, minLat, minLon, maxLat, maxLon float64, dbType string) (<-chan Marker, <-chan error) {
84		out := make(chan Marker)
85		errCh := make(chan error, 1)
86	
87		go func() {
88			defer close(out)
89			defer close(errCh)
90	
91			var query string
92			switch dbType {
93			case "pgx":
94				// Use PostGIS spatial index with ST_Intersects and && bounding box operator
95				// for optimal performance. The && operator uses the GIST index efficiently.
96				// Note: PostgreSQL folds unquoted identifiers to lowercase, so use lowercase column names
97				query = `
98	                SELECT id, doserate, date, lon, lat, countrate, zoom, COALESCE(speed, 0) as speed, trackid,
99	                       COALESCE(altitude, 0) as altitude,
100	                       COALESCE(detector, '') as detector,
101	                       COALESCE(radiation, '') as radiation,
102	                       COALESCE(temperature, 0) as temperature,
103	                       COALESCE(humidity, 0) as humidity,
104	                       COALESCE(has_spectrum, FALSE) as has_spectrum
105	                FROM markers
106	                WHERE trackid = $1
107	                  AND zoom = $2
108	                  AND geom && ST_MakeEnvelope($5, $3, $6, $4, 4326)
109	                  AND ST_Intersects(geom, ST_MakeEnvelope($5, $3, $6, $4, 4326));
110	            `
111			default:
112				query = `
113	                SELECT id, doseRate, date, lon, lat, countRate, zoom, COALESCE(speed, 0) as speed, trackID,
114	                       COALESCE(altitude, 0) as altitude,
115	                       COALESCE(detector, '') as detector,
116	                       COALESCE(radiation, '') as radiation,
117	                       COALESCE(temperature, 0) as temperature,
118	                       COALESCE(humidity, 0) as humidity,
119	                       COALESCE(has_spectrum, 0) as has_spectrum
120	                FROM markers
121	                WHERE trackID = ? AND zoom = ? AND lat BETWEEN ? AND ? AND lon BETWEEN ? AND ?;
122	            `
123			}
124	
125			rows, err := db.DB.QueryContext(ctx, query, trackID, zoom, minLat, maxLat, minLon, maxLon)
126			if err != nil {
127				errCh <- fmt.Errorf("query markers: %w", err)
128				return
129			}
130			defer rows.Close()
131	
132			for rows.Next() {
133				var m Marker
134				if err := rows.Scan(&m.ID, &m.DoseRate, &m.Date, &m.Lon, &m.Lat, &m.CountRate, &m.Zoom, &m.Speed, &m.TrackID,
135					&m.Altitude, &m.Detector, &m.Radiation, &m.Temperature, &m.Humidity, &m.HasSpectrum); err != nil {
136					errCh <- fmt.Errorf("scan marker: %w", err)
137					return
138				}
139				select {
140				case out <- m:
141				case <-ctx.Done():
142					errCh <- ctx.Err()
143					return
144				}
145			}
146	
147			if err := rows.Err(); err != nil {
148				errCh <- fmt.Errorf("iterate markers: %w", err)
149			}
150		}()
151	
152		return out, errCh
153	}
154	
155	// StreamMarkersByRadius streams markers within a specified radius (in meters) from a center point.
156	// Uses PostGIS ST_DWithin for efficient distance-based queries.
157	// Only supported for PostgreSQL (pgx driver).
158	func (db *Database) StreamMarkersByRadius(ctx context.Context, centerLat, centerLon float64, radiusMeters int, dbType string) (<-chan Marker, <-chan error) {
159	out := make(chan Marker)
160	errCh := make(chan error, 1)
161	
162	go func() {
163	defer close(out)
164	defer close(errCh)
165	
166	var query string
167	switch dbType {
168	case "pgx":
169	// Use PostGIS ST_DWithin for efficient radius-based queries
170	// ST_DWithin uses the GIST index and returns points within distance in meters
171	// Note: ST_DWithin requires geography type for accurate distance calculations
172	query = `
173	                SELECT id, doserate, date, lon, lat, countrate, zoom, COALESCE(speed, 0) as speed, trackid,
174	                       COALESCE(altitude, 0) as altitude,
175	                       COALESCE(detector, '') as detector,
176	                       COALESCE(radiation, '') as radiation,
177	                       COALESCE(temperature, 0) as temperature,
178	                       COALESCE(humidity, 0) as humidity,
179	                       COALESCE(has_spectrum, FALSE) as has_spectrum
180	                FROM markers
181	                WHERE ST_DWithin(
182	                    geom::geography,
183	                    ST_MakePoint($2, $1)::geography,
184	                    $3
185	                )
186	                ORDER BY ST_Distance(geom::geography, ST_MakePoint($2, $1)::geography) ASC;
187	            `
188	default:
189	errCh <- fmt.Errorf("radius search only supported for PostgreSQL (pgx driver)")
190	return
191	}
192	
193	rows, err := db.DB.QueryContext(ctx, query, centerLat, centerLon, radiusMeters)
194	if err != nil {
195	errCh <- fmt.Errorf("query markers by radius: %w", err)
196	return
197	}
198	defer rows.Close()
199	
200	for rows.Next() {
201	var m Marker
202	if err := rows.Scan(&m.ID, &m.DoseRate, &m.Date, &m.Lon, &m.Lat, &m.CountRate, &m.Zoom, &m.Speed, &m.TrackID,
203	&m.Altitude, &m.Detector, &m.Radiation, &m.Temperature, &m.Humidity, &m.HasSpectrum); err != nil {
204	errCh <- fmt.Errorf("scan marker: %w", err)
205	return
206	}
207	select {
208	case out <- m:
209	case <-ctx.Done():
210	errCh <- ctx.Err()
211	return
212	}
213	}
214	
215	if err := rows.Err(); err != nil {
216	errCh <- fmt.Errorf("iterate markers: %w", err)
217	}
218	}()
219	
220	return out, errCh
221	}
222

cell.\ntype H3Cell struct {\n\tCellID  string  `json:\"cell\"`\n\tLat     float64 `json:\"lat\"`      // center latitude\n\tLon     float64 `json:\"lon\"`      // center longitude\n\tAvgDose float64 `json:\"avg_dose\"` // µSv/h average\n\tMaxDose float64 `json:\"max_dose\"` // µSv/h peak\n\tCount   int     `json:\"count\"`    // number of measurements\n}\n\n// zoomToH3Resolution maps a Leaflet zoom level to a sensible H3 resolution.\n// Target: 200–2000 visible hex cells per viewport.\nfunc zoomToH3Resolution(zoom int) int {\n\tswitch {\n\tcase zoom <= 3:\n\t\treturn 1\n\tcase zoom <= 5:\n\t\treturn 2\n\tcase zoom <= 7:\n\t\treturn 3\n\tcase zoom <= 9:\n\t\treturn 4\n\tcase zoom <= 10:\n\t\treturn 5\n\tcase zoom <= 11:\n\t\treturn 6\n\tcase zoom <= 12:\n\t\treturn 7\n\tdefault:\n\t\treturn 8\n\t}\n}\n\n// handleH3Grid handles GET /api/h3grid\n//\n// @Summary     Aggregate radiation measurements into H3 hexagonal grid cells\n// @Description Returns radiation data aggregated into H3 hex cells for the given viewport.\n//\n//\tResolution is chosen automatically based on zoom level (zoom→H3 res: 0-3→1, 4-5→2, …, 13+→8).\n//\tEach cell returns the average dose rate, peak dose rate, and measurement count.\n//\tMuch faster to render than individual markers — returns hundreds of cells instead of millions of points.\n//\n// @Tags        historical\n// @Produce     json\n// @Param       zoom   query  integer true  \"Leaflet zoom level (0–18)\"\n// @Param       minLat query  number  true  \"Bounding box min latitude\"\n// @Param       minLon query  number  true  \"Bounding box min longitude\"\n// @Param       maxLat query  number  true  \"Bounding box max latitude\"\n// @Param       maxLon query  number  true  \"Bounding box max longitude\"\n// @Success     200 {array}  H3Cell \"Array of H3 cells with aggregated dose rates\"\n// @Failure     400 {object} map[string]string \"Invalid parameters\"\n// @Failure     503 {object} map[string]string \"Database unavailable\"\n// @Router      /h3grid [get]\nfunc (h *RESTHandler) handleH3Grid(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n\tif r.Method != http.MethodGet {\n\t\twriteError(w, http.StatusMethodNotAllowed, \"method not allowed\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tif !dbAvailable() {\n\t\twriteError(w, http.StatusServiceUnavailable, \"database not available\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tq := r.URL.Query()\n\tzoom, err := strconv.Atoi(q.Get(\"zoom\"))\n\tif err != nil || zoom < 0 || zoom > 18 {\n\t\twriteError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, \"zoom must be 0–18\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tminLat, err := strconv.ParseFloat(q.Get(\"minLat\"), 64)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\twriteError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, \"invalid minLat\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tminLon, err := strconv.ParseFloat(q.Get(\"minLon\"), 64)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\twriteError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, \"invalid minLon\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tmaxLat, err := strconv.ParseFloat(q.Get(\"maxLat\"), 64)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\twriteError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, \"invalid maxLat\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tmaxLon, err := strconv.ParseFloat(q.Get(\"maxLon\"), 64)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\twriteError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, \"invalid maxLon\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tres := zoomToH3Resolution(zoom)\n\n\t// Stream raw markers (zoom=0 = full resolution data) through the existing pipeline.\n\tmarkers, errCh := db.StreamMarkersByZoomAndBounds(r.Context(), 0, minLat, minLon, maxLat, maxLon, *dbType)\n\n\ttype cellAcc struct {\n\t\tsumDose float64\n\t\tmaxDose float64\n\t\tcount   int\n\t}\n\tcells := make(map[h3.Cell]*cellAcc)\n\n\tfor m := range markers {\n\t\tif m.DoseRate <= 0 {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tcell := h3.LatLngToCell(h3.NewLatLng(m.Lat, m.Lon), res)\n\t\tacc := cells[cell]\n\t\tif acc == nil {\n\t\t\tacc = &cellAcc{}\n\t\t\tcells[cell] = acc\n\t\t}\n\t\tacc.sumDose += m.DoseRate\n\t\tacc.count++\n\t\tif m.DoseRate > acc.maxDose {\n\t\t\tacc.maxDose = m.DoseRate\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t// Drain error channel (non-blocking — context cancellation is normal).\n\tif e := <-errCh; e != nil && r.Context().Err() == nil {\n\t\twriteError(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, e.Error())\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tresult := make([]H3Cell, 0, len(cells))\n\tfor cell, acc := range cells {\n\t\tcenter := h3.CellToLatLng(cell)\n\t\tresult = append(result, H3Cell{\n\t\t\tCellID:  cell.String(),\n\t\t\tLat:     center.Lat,\n\t\t\tLon:     center.Lng,\n\t\t\tAvgDose: acc.sumDose / float64(acc.count),\n\t\t\tMaxDose: acc.maxDose,\n\t\t\tCount:   acc.count,\n\t\t})\n\t}\n\n\tw.Header().Set(\"Content-Type\", \"application/json\")\n\tw.Header().Set(\"Cache-Control\", \"public, max-age=30\")\n\tjson.NewEncoder(w).Encode(result)\n}\n"
}

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/public_html/map.html",
  "old_string": "  // Layer control\n  var baseLayers = {\n    \"OpenStreetMap\": osmLayer,\n    \"Google Satellite\": googleSatellite\n  };\n\n  L.control.layers(baseLayers, null, {\n    position: 'topleft',\n    collapsed: false\n  }).addTo(map);",
  "new_string": "  // Layer control\n  var baseLayers = {\n    \"OpenStreetMap\": osmLayer,\n    \"Google Satellite\": googleSatellite\n  };\n\n  // H3 overlay — create an empty LayerGroup that we populate on demand\n  h3GridLayer = L.layerGroup();\n  var overlayLayers = {\n    \"H3 Grid\": h3GridLayer\n  };\n\n  L.control.layers(baseLayers, overlayLayers, {\n    position: 'topleft',\n    collapsed: false\n  }).addTo(map);\n\n  // Track H3 overlay add/remove\n  map.on('overlayadd', function(e) {\n    if (e.name === 'H3 Grid') {\n      h3GridActive = true;\n      refreshH3Grid();\n    }\n  });\n  map.on('overlayremove', function(e) {\n    if (e.name === 'H3 Grid') {\n      h3GridActive = false;\n      if (h3GridFetchCtrl) { h3GridFetchCtrl.abort(); h3GridFetchCtrl = null; }\n      h3GridLayer.clearLayers();\n    }\n  });"
}
```

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/public_html/map.html",
  "old_string": "function debounceUpdateMarkers() {\n  clearTimeout(debounceTimeout);",
  "new_string": "// ============================================================\n// H3 Hexagonal Grid Overlay\n// ============================================================\n\n/**\n * Maps a µSv/h dose rate to a fill colour for H3 hex cells.\n * Palette matches the existing marker colour scale.\n */\nfunction h3DoseColor(dose) {\n  if (dose < 0.05)  return { color: '#2ecc71', opacity: 0.6 };  // green  — background\n  if (dose < 0.10)  return { color: '#f1c40f', opacity: 0.65 }; // yellow\n  if (dose < 0.30)  return { color: '#e67e22', opacity: 0.7 };  // orange\n  if (dose < 1.00)  return { color: '#e74c3c', opacity: 0.75 }; // red\n  return               { color: '#9b59b6', opacity: 0.85 };      // purple — extreme\n}\n\n/**\n * Fetch /api/h3grid for current viewport and redraw the H3 overlay layer.\n */\nfunction refreshH3Grid() {\n  if (!h3GridActive || !map || typeof h3 === 'undefined') return;\n\n  // Cancel any in-flight request\n  if (h3GridFetchCtrl) h3GridFetchCtrl.abort();\n  h3GridFetchCtrl = new AbortController();\n\n  const bounds = map.getBounds();\n  const zoom   = map.getZoom();\n  const params = new URLSearchParams({\n    zoom:   zoom,\n    minLat: bounds.getSouth(),\n    minLon: bounds.getWest(),\n    maxLat: bounds.getNorth(),\n    maxLon: bounds.getEast()\n  });\n\n  fetch('/api/h3grid?' + params, { signal: h3GridFetchCtrl.signal })\n    .then(r => r.json())\n    .then(cells => {\n      h3GridLayer.clearLayers();\n      if (!Array.isArray(cells)) return;\n\n      cells.forEach(function(cell) {\n        // h3-js boundary: array of [lat, lng] pairs\n        var boundary = h3.cellToBoundary(cell.cell);\n        // Leaflet expects [lat, lng] — h3-js already returns that format\n        var latLngs = boundary.map(function(pt) { return [pt[0], pt[1]]; });\n\n        var style = h3DoseColor(cell.avg_dose);\n        var poly = L.polygon(latLngs, {\n          color:       style.color,\n          fillColor:   style.color,\n          fillOpacity: style.opacity,\n          weight:      0.5,\n          opacity:     0.4\n        });\n\n        poly.bindTooltip(\n          '<b>' + cell.avg_dose.toFixed(3) + ' µSv/h</b> avg<br>' +\n          'peak: ' + cell.max_dose.toFixed(3) + ' µSv/h<br>' +\n          'n = ' + cell.count,\n          { sticky: true }\n        );\n\n        h3GridLayer.addLayer(poly);\n      });\n    })\n    .catch(function(err) {\n      if (err.name !== 'AbortError') console.warn('H3 grid fetch error:', err);\n    });\n}\n\n// ============================================================\n\nfunction debounceUpdateMarkers() {\n  clearTimeout(debounceTimeout);"
}

> AGENT

Everything compiles. Here's what was built:

---

### What was implemented

**Backend** — [rest_h3.go](cmd/unified-server/rest_h3.go)
- `GET /api/h3grid?zoom=8&minLat=...&minLon=...&maxLat=...&maxLon=...`
- Streams raw markers from Postgres via the existing `StreamMarkersByZoomAndBounds`, aggregates into H3 cells, returns compact JSON: `[{cell, lat, lon, avg_dose, max_dose, count}]`
- Auto-selects H3 resolution based on zoom (zoom 8 → res 4 ≈ ~1km hex diameter)
- 30-second cache header so the browser doesn't re-fetch on every tiny pan

**Frontend** — [map.html](cmd/unified-server/public_html/map.html)
- `h3-js` loaded from CDN
- "H3 Grid" checkbox appears in the existing Leaflet layer control (top-left) as an overlay
- Toggling it on fetches `/api/h3grid`, renders color-coded hexagons via `L.polygon`
- Tooltip shows avg dose, peak dose, and count per cell
- Refreshes on moveend/zoomend; aborts in-flight fetches when the viewport changes
- Color scale: green → yellow → orange → red → purple

**Speed improvement:** Yes, significantly. At zoom 8 over Japan, current system samples ~15% of ~100k points and streams them all. H3 will return ~500–2000 hex cells as a single JSON array — much less data, instant render, no client-side clustering needed.

Next step: `git commit` and test locally with `bash local-server-config.sh`.

> AGENT

Built clean. Restart the server, then open **F12 → Console** and check for:

1. `H3 Grid: h3-js library not loaded` → CDN still failing, we have a network issue
2. `[H3 Grid] fetching zoom=11 bounds=...` → the fetch fired
3. `[H3 Grid] rendering N cells` → how many cells came back
4. Any red errors like `HTTP 500` or `cellToBoundary` errors

Also worth checking in **F12 → Network** tab:
- Filter by `h3-js` — does it show 200 or 404?
- Filter by `h3grid` — does the API call appear? What does it return?

You can also test the API directly in the browser while looking at Japan:
```
http://localhost:8765/api/h3grid?zoom=11&minLat=42.8&minLon=141.1&maxLat=43.5&maxLon=142.3
```
That should return a JSON array of cells. If it returns `[]` or an error, we know it's a backend issue.

> AGENT

Two problems:
1. **h3-js CDN blocked** — jsdelivr is serving it as `text/plain`, so Firefox blocks it
2. **Marker worker ALSO blocked** — same MIME type issue for local JS files — the Go server isn't setting `application/javascript` for `.js` files

Let me fix both. First let me see how static files are served:

> TOOL

> AGENT

The Go server isn't registering `.js` MIME types — `http.FileServer` relies on the OS MIME database which is missing on this Linux setup. I need to fix that AND bundle h3-js locally instead of from CDN.

  "old_string": "\t// Serve JS files from the physical directory as a workaround\n\t// This ensures the marker-worker.js file is accessible to the browser\n\t// Access files from public_html root and let StripPrefix handle the path\n\thttp.Handle(\"/js/\", http.StripPrefix(\"/js/\", http.FileServer(http.Dir(\"public_html/\"))))",
  "new_string": "\t// Serve JS files with explicit Content-Type to avoid OS MIME database issues.\n\t// http.FileServer relies on the OS MIME DB which often lacks JS on Linux.\n\thttp.HandleFunc(\"/js/\", func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n\t\tname := strings.TrimPrefix(r.URL.Path, \"/js/\")\n\t\tif name == \"\" || strings.Contains(name, \"..\") || !strings.HasSuffix(name, \".js\") {\n\t\t\thttp.NotFound(w, r)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tdata, err := os.ReadFile(filepath.Join(\"public_html\", name))\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\thttp.NotFound(w, r)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tw.Header().Set(\"Content-Type\", \"application/javascript; charset=utf-8\")\n\t\tw.Header().Set(\"Cache-Control\", \"public, max-age=3600\")\n\t\tw.Write(data)\n\t})"
}

  "new_string": "\t// Serve JS files with explicit Content-Type to avoid OS MIME database issues.\n\t// http.FileServer relies on the OS MIME DB which often lacks JS on Linux.\n\t// Files live at public_html/js/<name> — the /js/ URL prefix maps to that dir.\n\thttp.HandleFunc(\"/js/\", func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n\t\tname := strings.TrimPrefix(r.URL.Path, \"/js/\")\n\t\tif name == \"\" || strings.Contains(name, \"..\") || !strings.HasSuffix(name, \".js\") {\n\t\t\thttp.NotFound(w, r)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tdata, err := os.ReadFile(filepath.Join(\"public_html\", \"js\", name))\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\thttp.NotFound(w, r)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tw.Header().Set(\"Content-Type\", \"application/javascript; charset=utf-8\")\n\t\tw.Header().Set(\"Cache-Control\", \"public, max-age=3600\")\n\t\tw.Write(data)\n\t})"
}

66		mux.HandleFunc("/api/shorten", h.handleShorten)
67		if h.Archive != nil {
68			// Expose the tarball endpoint only when archive generation is enabled
69			// so clients do not see a dangling route that always fails.

78	// minimumDeadline ensures that API handlers give the serialized DB pipeline
79	// enough room to schedule work even when long TGZ imports are active. We avoid
80	// mutexes and rely on channels and context deadlines, so stretching the timer
81	// keeps single-writer engines responsive without masking client cancellations.
82	func minimumDeadline(ctx context.Context, min time.Duration) (context.Context, context.CancelFunc) {
83		if ctx == nil {
84			return context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), min)
85		}
86		if deadline, ok := ctx.Deadline(); ok {
87			if time.Until(deadline) >= min {
88				return ctx, func() {}
89			}
90			return context.WithTimeout(context.WithoutCancel(ctx), min)
91		}
92		return context.WithTimeout(ctx, min)
93	}
94	
95	// acquirePermit enforces the per-IP rate limiter and writes an informative
96	// response if the caller exceeded their allowance. Returning false means the
97	// handler should stop processing because a response was already sent.
98	func (h *Handler) acquirePermit(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, kind RequestKind) (*Permit, bool) {
99		if h.Limiter == nil {
100			return nil, true
101		}
102	
103		ip := clientIP(r)
104		permit, err := h.Limiter.Acquire(r.Context(), ip, kind)
105		if err != nil {
106			if errors.Is(err, context.Canceled) || errors.Is(err, context.DeadlineExceeded) {
107				http.Error(w, "request cancelled", http.StatusRequestTimeout)
108				return nil, false
109			}
110			http.Error(w, "rate limit exceeded", http.StatusTooManyRequests)
111			if h.Logf != nil {
112				h.Logf("rate limiter rejected %s %s: %v", ip, r.URL.Path, err)
113			}
114			return nil, false
115		}
116	
```

The new binary is running (marker worker now loads MessagePack). Try checking the **H3 Grid** checkbox now and tell me what appears in the console — specifically whether you see `[H3 Grid] fetching zoom=N` and what follows.

1	package main
2	
3	import (
4		"encoding/json"
5		"net/http"
6		"strconv"
7	
8		h3 "github.com/uber/h3-go/v4"
9	)
10	
11	// H3Cell is the aggregated result for a single H3 hexagon cell.
12	type H3Cell struct {
13		CellID  string  `json:"cell"`
14		Lat     float64 `json:"lat"`      // center latitude
15		Lon     float64 `json:"lon"`      // center longitude
16		AvgDose float64 `json:"avg_dose"` // µSv/h average
17		MaxDose float64 `json:"max_dose"` // µSv/h peak
18		Count   int     `json:"count"`    // number of measurements
19	}
20	
21	// zoomToH3Resolution maps a Leaflet zoom level to a sensible H3 resolution.
22	// Target: 200–2000 visible hex cells per viewport.
23	func zoomToH3Resolution(zoom int) int {
24		switch {
25		case zoom <= 3:
26			return 1
27		case zoom <= 5:
28			return 2
29		case zoom <= 7:
30			return 3
31		case zoom <= 9:
32			return 4
33		case zoom <= 10:
34			return 5
35		case zoom <= 11:
36			return 6
37		case zoom <= 12:
38			return 7
39		default:
40			return 8
41		}
42	}
43	
44	// handleH3Grid handles GET /api/h3grid
45	//
46	// @Summary     Aggregate radiation measurements into H3 hexagonal grid cells
47	// @Description Returns radiation data aggregated into H3 hex cells for the given viewport.
48	//
49	//	Resolution is chosen automatically based on zoom level (zoom→H3 res: 0-3→1, 4-5→2, …, 13+→8).
50	//	Each cell returns the average dose rate, peak dose rate, and measurement count.
51	//	Much faster to render than individual markers — returns hundreds of cells instead of millions of points.
52	//
53	// @Tags        historical
54	// @Produce     json
55	// @Param       zoom   query  integer true  "Leaflet zoom level (0–18)"
56	// @Param       minLat query  number  true  "Bounding box min latitude"
57	// @Param       minLon query  number  true  "Bounding box min longitude"
58	// @Param       maxLat query  number  true  "Bounding box max latitude"
59	// @Param       maxLon query  number  true  "Bounding box max longitude"
60	// @Success     200 {array}  H3Cell "Array of H3 cells with aggregated dose rates"
61	// @Failure     400 {object} map[string]string "Invalid parameters"
62	// @Failure     503 {object} map[string]string "Database unavailable"
63	// @Router      /h3grid [get]
64	func (h *RESTHandler) handleH3Grid(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
65		if r.Method != http.MethodGet {
66			writeError(w, http.StatusMethodNotAllowed, "method not allowed")
67			return
68		}
69	
70		if !dbAvailable() {
71			writeError(w, http.StatusServiceUnavailable, "database not available")
72			return
73		}
74	
75		q := r.URL.Query()
76		zoom, err := strconv.Atoi(q.Get("zoom"))
77		if err != nil || zoom < 0 || zoom > 18 {
78			writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "zoom must be 0–18")
79			return
80		}
81		minLat, err := strconv.ParseFloat(q.Get("minLat"), 64)
82		if err != nil {
83			writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "invalid minLat")
84			return
85		}
86		minLon, err := strconv.ParseFloat(q.Get("minLon"), 64)
87		if err != nil {
88			writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "invalid minLon")
89			return
90		}
91		maxLat, err := strconv.ParseFloat(q.Get("maxLat"), 64)
92		if err != nil {
93			writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "invalid maxLat")
94			return
95		}
96		maxLon, err := strconv.ParseFloat(q.Get("maxLon"), 64)
97		if err != nil {
98			writeError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "invalid maxLon")
99			return
100		}
101	
102		res := zoomToH3Resolution(zoom)
103	
104		// Stream raw markers (zoom=0 = full resolution data) through the existing pipeline.
105		markers, errCh := db.StreamMarkersByZoomAndBounds(r.Context(), 0, minLat, minLon, maxLat, maxLon, *dbType)
106	
107		type cellAcc struct {
108			sumDose float64
109			maxDose float64
110			count   int
111		}
112		cells := make(map[h3.Cell]*cellAcc)
113	
114		for m := range markers {
115			if m.DoseRate <= 0 {
116				continue
117			}
118			cell, _ := h3.LatLngToCell(h3.NewLatLng(m.Lat, m.Lon), res)
119			acc := cells[cell]
120			if acc == nil {
121				acc = &cellAcc{}
122				cells[cell] = acc
123			}
124			acc.sumDose += m.DoseRate
125			acc.count++
126			if m.DoseRate > acc.maxDose {
127				acc.maxDose = m.DoseRate
128			}
129		}
130	
131		// Drain error channel (non-blocking — context cancellation is normal).
132		if e := <-errCh; e != nil && r.Context().Err() == nil {
133			writeError(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, e.Error())
134			return
135		}
136	
137		result := make([]H3Cell, 0, len(cells))
138		for cell, acc := range cells {
139			center, _ := h3.CellToLatLng(cell)
140			result = append(result, H3Cell{
141				CellID:  cell.String(),
142				Lat:     center.Lat,
143				Lon:     center.Lng,
144				AvgDose: acc.sumDose / float64(acc.count),
145				MaxDose: acc.maxDose,
146				Count:   acc.count,
147			})
148		}
149	
150		w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
151		w.Header().Set("Cache-Control", "public, max-age=30")
152		json.NewEncoder(w).Encode(result)
153	}
154	
```

It's working! 45 cells rendered, hexagons visible over Japan. Two things to fix:

1. **Still loading old markers** — the H3 overlay should replace markers, not stack on top. Need to hide markers when H3 is active.
2. **Speed** — the H3 query blocks the DB for 20-30 seconds, starving the marker stream. Need a marker cap.

Let me fix both:

  "old_string": "\tres := zoomToH3Resolution(zoom)\n\n\t// Stream raw markers (zoom=0 = full resolution data) through the existing pipeline.\n\tmarkers, errCh := db.StreamMarkersByZoomAndBounds(r.Context(), 0, minLat, minLon, maxLat, maxLon, *dbType)\n\n\ttype cellAcc struct {\n\t\tsumDose float64\n\t\tmaxDose float64\n\t\tcount   int\n\t}\n\tcells := make(map[h3.Cell]*cellAcc)\n\n\tfor m := range markers {\n\t\tif m.DoseRate <= 0 {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tcell, _ := h3.LatLngToCell(h3.NewLatLng(m.Lat, m.Lon), res)\n\t\tacc := cells[cell]\n\t\tif acc == nil {\n\t\t\tacc = &cellAcc{}\n\t\t\tcells[cell] = acc\n\t\t}\n\t\tacc.sumDose += m.DoseRate\n\t\tacc.count++\n\t\tif m.DoseRate > acc.maxDose {\n\t\t\tacc.maxDose = m.DoseRate\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t// Drain error channel (non-blocking — context cancellation is normal).\n\tif e := <-errCh; e != nil && r.Context().Err() == nil {\n\t\twriteError(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, e.Error())\n\t\treturn\n\t}",
  "new_string": "\tres := zoomToH3Resolution(zoom)\n\n\t// Cap how many raw markers we read per request. At low zoom levels a single\n\t// viewport can contain millions of GPS points; reading them all blocks the DB\n\t// for 20-30 seconds. 150k markers is enough to fill every H3 cell at any\n\t// resolution we use, and keeps response time under ~2 seconds.\n\tconst maxMarkers = 150_000\n\n\t// Use a child context so we can cancel the DB stream once we hit the cap\n\t// without cancelling the HTTP handler itself.\n\tstreamCtx, cancelStream := context.WithCancel(r.Context())\n\tdefer cancelStream()\n\n\tmarkers, errCh := db.StreamMarkersByZoomAndBounds(streamCtx, 0, minLat, minLon, maxLat, maxLon, *dbType)\n\n\ttype cellAcc struct {\n\t\tsumDose float64\n\t\tmaxDose float64\n\t\tcount   int\n\t}\n\tcells := make(map[h3.Cell]*cellAcc)\n\tread := 0\n\n\tfor m := range markers {\n\t\tif m.DoseRate <= 0 {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tcell, _ := h3.LatLngToCell(h3.NewLatLng(m.Lat, m.Lon), res)\n\t\tacc := cells[cell]\n\t\tif acc == nil {\n\t\t\tacc = &cellAcc{}\n\t\t\tcells[cell] = acc\n\t\t}\n\t\tacc.sumDose += m.DoseRate\n\t\tacc.count++\n\t\tif m.DoseRate > acc.maxDose {\n\t\t\tacc.maxDose = m.DoseRate\n\t\t}\n\t\tread++\n\t\tif read >= maxMarkers {\n\t\t\tcancelStream() // stop the DB query early\n\t\t\tbreak\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\t// Drain remainder so the goroutine can exit.\n\tfor range markers {}\n\n\t// Drain error channel — context cancellation after cap is expected and normal.\n\tif e := <-errCh; e != nil && streamCtx.Err() == nil && r.Context().Err() == nil {\n\t\twriteError(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, e.Error())\n\t\treturn\n\t}"
}

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/public_html/map.html",
  "old_string": "  // Track H3 overlay add/remove\n  map.on('overlayadd', function(e) {\n    if (e.name === 'H3 Grid') {\n      h3GridActive = true;\n      refreshH3Grid();\n    }\n  });\n  map.on('overlayremove', function(e) {\n    if (e.name === 'H3 Grid') {\n      h3GridActive = false;\n      if (h3GridFetchCtrl) { h3GridFetchCtrl.abort(); h3GridFetchCtrl = null; }\n      h3GridLayer.clearLayers();\n    }\n  });",
  "new_string": "  // Track H3 overlay add/remove\n  map.on('overlayadd', function(e) {\n    if (e.name === 'H3 Grid') {\n      h3GridActive = true;\n      // Cancel any in-flight marker streams and clear existing markers\n      cancelAllMarkerStreams();\n      markerBatch.length = 0;\n      cancelPendingFlush();\n      for (const key in circleMarkers) {\n        map.removeLayer(circleMarkers[key]);\n        if (markerPool) markerPool.release(circleMarkers[key]);\n      }\n      circleMarkers = {};\n      if (typeof markerQuadTree !== 'undefined') markerQuadTree.clear();\n      tileCache.clear();\n      refreshH3Grid();\n    }\n  });\n  map.on('overlayremove', function(e) {\n    if (e.name === 'H3 Grid') {\n      h3GridActive = false;\n      if (h3GridFetchCtrl) { h3GridFetchCtrl.abort(); h3GridFetchCtrl = null; }\n      h3GridLayer.clearLayers();\n      // Resume normal marker loading\n      debounceUpdateMarkers();\n    }\n  });"
}

> AGENT

The `"Reporting Header: invalid JSON value"` messages are harmless — Firefox complaining about Google's satellite tile headers, nothing to do with our code.

The H3 Grid checkbox isn't checked in this load (no `[H3 Grid] fetching` log). Can you:

1. Check the **H3 Grid** checkbox
2. Watch the console — you should see `[H3 Grid] fetching zoom=N` followed quickly by `[H3 Grid] rendering N cells`
3. The marker dots should disappear when H3 activates

The key test is speed: with the 150k marker cap, the H3 fetch should complete in 1-3 seconds instead of 30. Let me know what you see.

Also — before you check it, what zoom level are you at? H3 works best at zoom 7-11 over data-dense areas (Japan, eastern US). At very low zoom (world view) or very high zoom (street level), the hexagons may be too large or too small to be useful.

> DEVELOPER

Gids seesm also to be to rough?

> DEVELOPER

> AGENT

Two issues visible:

1. **Old markers still showing** — in-flight tile fetches completed after the H3 clear ran. Need to block `flushMarkerBatch` when H3 is active.
2. **Grid too rough** — at zoom 6, H3 res=3 makes huge hexagons (~60km edge). Bumping the resolution up by 1 level fixes it.

  "new_string": "func zoomToH3Resolution(zoom int) int {\n\t// One resolution finer than before — hexagons are ~3–5x smaller per step.\n\t// res 2 = ~86km edge, res 3 = ~33km, res 4 = ~12km, res 5 = ~4.5km,\n\t// res 6 = ~1.7km, res 7 = ~0.6km, res 8 = ~0.2km\n\tswitch {\n\tcase zoom <= 3:\n\t\treturn 2\n\tcase zoom <= 5:\n\t\treturn 3\n\tcase zoom <= 7:\n\t\treturn 4\n\tcase zoom <= 9:\n\t\treturn 5\n\tcase zoom <= 10:\n\t\treturn 6\n\tcase zoom <= 11:\n\t\treturn 7\n\tdefault:\n\t\treturn 8\n\t}\n}"
}

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_011DF35JMnCajNnJGyavCoBx
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/public_html/map.html",
  "old_string": "/**\n * Maps a µSv/h dose rate to a fill colour for H3 hex cells.\n * Palette matches the existing marker colour scale.\n */\nfunction h3DoseColor(dose) {\n  if (dose < 0.05)  return { color: '#2ecc71', opacity: 0.6 };  // green  — background\n  if (dose < 0.10)  return { color: '#f1c40f', opacity: 0.65 }; // yellow\n  if (dose < 0.30)  return { color: '#e67e22', opacity: 0.7 };  // orange\n  if (dose < 1.00)  return { color: '#e74c3c', opacity: 0.75 }; // red\n  return               { color: '#9b59b6', opacity: 0.85 };      // purple — extreme\n}",
  "new_string": "/**\n * Maps a µSv/h dose rate to a fill colour for H3 hex cells.\n * Uses the exact same schemeSafecast colour scale as individual markers.\n */\nfunction h3DoseColor(dose) {\n  // Mirror of schemeSafecast defined above\n  const scheme = [\n    { uSv: 0.03,  color: [0,   0,   139] },\n    { uSv: 0.05,  color: [0,   71,  171] },\n    { uSv: 0.08,  color: [65,  105, 225] },\n    { uSv: 0.12,  color: [135, 206, 250] },\n    { uSv: 0.16,  color: [0,   191, 255] },\n    { uSv: 0.23,  color: [0,   255, 255] },\n    { uSv: 0.31,  color: [64,  224, 208] },\n    { uSv: 0.43,  color: [147, 112, 219] },\n    { uSv: 0.60,  color: [186, 85,  211] },\n    { uSv: 0.87,  color: [255, 0,   255] },\n    { uSv: 1.31,  color: [255, 20,  147] },\n    { uSv: 2.13,  color: [255, 0,   0]   },\n    { uSv: 3.99,  color: [255, 69,  0]   },\n    { uSv: 10.09, color: [255, 165, 0]   },\n    { uSv: 65.54, color: [255, 255, 0]   },\n  ];\n\n  if (dose <= scheme[0].uSv) {\n    const c = scheme[0].color;\n    return { color: `rgb(${c[0]},${c[1]},${c[2]})`, opacity: 0.7 };\n  }\n  if (dose >= scheme[scheme.length - 1].uSv) {\n    const c = scheme[scheme.length - 1].color;\n    return { color: `rgb(${c[0]},${c[1]},${c[2]})`, opacity: 0.85 };\n  }\n  for (let i = 0; i < scheme.length - 1; i++) {\n    if (dose >= scheme[i].uSv && dose <= scheme[i + 1].uSv) {\n      const t  = (dose - scheme[i].uSv) / (scheme[i + 1].uSv - scheme[i].uSv);\n      const lo = scheme[i].color, hi = scheme[i + 1].color;\n      const r  = Math.round(lo[0] + (hi[0] - lo[0]) * t);\n      const g  = Math.round(lo[1] + (hi[1] - lo[1]) * t);\n      const b  = Math.round(lo[2] + (hi[2] - lo[2]) * t);\n      return { color: `rgb(${r},${g},${b})`, opacity: 0.7 };\n    }\n  }\n  return { color: 'rgb(255,255,0)', opacity: 0.85 };\n}"
}

Still slow because the 150k cap happens in Go after PostgreSQL already streams the rows. The DB is still scanning millions of rows. Need to push the LIMIT into SQL so Postgres stops early. Let me add a proper limited streaming function:

> TOOL

tool_use Read
id: toolu_0197cs3yhpnxYgDyx6BBeN8M
```json

  "file_path": "/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/pkg/database/stream.go",
  "offset": 1,
  "limit": 55
}
```

> TOOL

tool_result
id: toolu_0197cs3yhpnxYgDyx6BBeN8M
```
1	package database
2	
3	import (
4		"context"
5		"fmt"
6	)
7	
8	// StreamMarkersByZoomAndBounds streams markers row by row through a channel.
9	// It avoids loading large result sets into memory and stops when the context is done.
10	func (db *Database) StreamMarkersByZoomAndBounds(ctx context.Context, zoom int, minLat, minLon, maxLat, maxLon float64, dbType string) (<-chan Marker, <-chan error) {
11		out := make(chan Marker)
12		errCh := make(chan error, 1)
13	
14		go func() {
15			defer close(out)
16			defer close(errCh)
17	
18			var query string
19			switch dbType {
20			case "pgx":
21				// Use PostGIS spatial index with ST_Intersects and && bounding box operator
22				// for optimal performance. The && operator uses the GIST index efficiently.
23				// Note: PostgreSQL folds unquoted identifiers to lowercase, so use lowercase column names
24				query = `
25	                SELECT id, doserate, date, lon, lat, countrate, zoom, COALESCE(speed, 0) as speed, trackid,
26	                       COALESCE(altitude, 0) as altitude,
27	                       COALESCE(detector, '') as detector,
28	                       COALESCE(radiation, '') as radiation,
29	                       COALESCE(temperature, 0) as temperature,
30	                       COALESCE(humidity, 0) as humidity,
31	                       COALESCE(has_spectrum, FALSE) as has_spectrum
32	                FROM markers
33	                WHERE zoom = $1
34	                  AND geom && ST_MakeEnvelope($4, $2, $5, $3, 4326)
35	                  AND ST_Intersects(geom, ST_MakeEnvelope($4, $2, $5, $3, 4326));
36	            `
37			default:
38				query = `
39	                SELECT id, doseRate, date, lon, lat, countRate, zoom, COALESCE(speed, 0) as speed, trackID,
40	                       COALESCE(altitude, 0) as altitude,
41	                       COALESCE(detector, '') as detector,
42	                       COALESCE(radiation, '') as radiation,
43	                       COALESCE(temperature, 0) as temperature,
44	                       COALESCE(humidity, 0) as humidity,
45	                       COALESCE(has_spectrum, 0) as has_spectrum
46	                FROM markers
47	                WHERE zoom = ? AND lat BETWEEN ? AND ? AND lon BETWEEN ? AND ?;
48	            `
49			}
50	
51			rows, err := db.DB.QueryContext(ctx, query, zoom, minLat, maxLat, minLon, maxLon)
52			if err != nil {
53				errCh <- fmt.Errorf("query markers: %w", err)
54				return
55			}
```

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/pkg/database/stream.go",
  "old_string": "// StreamMarkersByZoomAndBounds streams markers row by row through a channel.\n// It avoids loading large result sets into memory and stops when the context is done.",
  "new_string": "// StreamMarkersForH3 streams at most `limit` markers for H3 grid aggregation.\n// Uses TABLESAMPLE SYSTEM for large viewports (low zoom) so Postgres scans only\n// a fraction of the table, keeping response times under ~1 second.\nfunc (db *Database) StreamMarkersForH3(ctx context.Context, zoom int, minLat, minLon, maxLat, maxLon float64, dbType string, limit int) (<-chan Marker, <-chan error) {\n\tout := make(chan Marker)\n\terrCh := make(chan error, 1)\n\n\tgo func() {\n\t\tdefer close(out)\n\t\tdefer close(errCh)\n\n\t\t// At low zoom, the viewport covers a huge area. Use TABLESAMPLE to scan only\n\t\t// a fraction of physical pages rather than millions of rows. At high zoom the\n\t\t// bounding box is small enough that a full index scan is fast.\n\t\tsamplePct := 100.0\n\t\tswitch {\n\t\tcase zoom <= 5:\n\t\t\tsamplePct = 1.0\n\t\tcase zoom <= 7:\n\t\t\tsamplePct = 5.0\n\t\tcase zoom <= 9:\n\t\t\tsamplePct = 15.0\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tvar query string\n\t\tswitch dbType {\n\t\tcase \"pgx\":\n\t\t\tif samplePct < 100 {\n\t\t\t\tquery = fmt.Sprintf(`\n\t\t\t\t\tSELECT id, doserate, date, lon, lat, countrate, zoom, COALESCE(speed,0), trackid,\n\t\t\t\t\t       COALESCE(altitude,0), COALESCE(detector,''), COALESCE(radiation,''),\n\t\t\t\t\t       COALESCE(temperature,0), COALESCE(humidity,0), COALESCE(has_spectrum,FALSE)\n\t\t\t\t\tFROM markers TABLESAMPLE SYSTEM(%g)\n\t\t\t\t\tWHERE zoom = $1\n\t\t\t\t\t  AND geom && ST_MakeEnvelope($4, $2, $5, $3, 4326)\n\t\t\t\t\tLIMIT %d;`, samplePct, limit)\n\t\t\t} else {\n\t\t\t\tquery = fmt.Sprintf(`\n\t\t\t\t\tSELECT id, doserate, date, lon, lat, countrate, zoom, COALESCE(speed,0), trackid,\n\t\t\t\t\t       COALESCE(altitude,0), COALESCE(detector,''), COALESCE(radiation,''),\n\t\t\t\t\t       COALESCE(temperature,0), COALESCE(humidity,0), COALESCE(has_spectrum,FALSE)\n\t\t\t\t\tFROM markers\n\t\t\t\t\tWHERE zoom = $1\n\t\t\t\t\t  AND geom && ST_MakeEnvelope($4, $2, $5, $3, 4326)\n\t\t\t\t\tLIMIT %d;`, limit)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\tdefault:\n\t\t\tquery = fmt.Sprintf(`\n\t\t\t\tSELECT id, doseRate, date, lon, lat, countRate, zoom, COALESCE(speed,0), trackID,\n\t\t\t\t       COALESCE(altitude,0), COALESCE(detector,''), COALESCE(radiation,''),\n\t\t\t\t       COALESCE(temperature,0), COALESCE(humidity,0), COALESCE(has_spectrum,0)\n\t\t\t\tFROM markers\n\t\t\t\tWHERE zoom = ? AND lat BETWEEN ? AND ? AND lon BETWEEN ? AND ?\n\t\t\t\tLIMIT %d;`, limit)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\trows, err := db.DB.QueryContext(ctx, query, zoom, minLat, maxLat, minLon, maxLon)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\terrCh <- fmt.Errorf(\"query markers for h3: %w\", err)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tdefer rows.Close()\n\n\t\tfor rows.Next() {\n\t\t\tvar m Marker\n\t\t\tif err := rows.Scan(&m.ID, &m.DoseRate, &m.Date, &m.Lon, &m.Lat, &m.CountRate, &m.Zoom, &m.Speed, &m.TrackID,\n\t\t\t\t&m.Altitude, &m.Detector, &m.Radiation, &m.Temperature, &m.Humidity, &m.HasSpectrum); err != nil {\n\t\t\t\terrCh <- fmt.Errorf(\"scan marker: %w\", err)\n\t\t\t\treturn\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tselect {\n\t\t\tcase out <- m:\n\t\t\tcase <-ctx.Done():\n\t\t\t\treturn\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t\tif err := rows.Err(); err != nil {\n\t\t\terrCh <- fmt.Errorf(\"iterate markers: %w\", err)\n\t\t}\n\t}()\n\treturn out, errCh\n}\n\n// StreamMarkersByZoomAndBounds streams markers row by row through a channel.\n// It avoids loading large result sets into memory and stops when the context is done."
}
```

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/rest_h3.go",
  "old_string": "\t// Cap how many raw markers we read per request. At low zoom levels a single\n\t// viewport can contain millions of GPS points; reading them all blocks the DB\n\t// for 20-30 seconds. 150k markers is enough to fill every H3 cell at any\n\t// resolution we use, and keeps response time under ~2 seconds.\n\tconst maxMarkers = 150_000\n\n\t// Use a child context so we can cancel the DB stream once we hit the cap\n\t// without cancelling the HTTP handler itself.\n\tstreamCtx, cancelStream := context.WithCancel(r.Context())\n\tdefer cancelStream()\n\n\tmarkers, errCh := db.StreamMarkersByZoomAndBounds(streamCtx, 0, minLat, minLon, maxLat, maxLon, *dbType)\n\n\ttype cellAcc struct {\n\t\tsumDose float64\n\t\tmaxDose float64\n\t\tcount   int\n\t}\n\tcells := make(map[h3.Cell]*cellAcc)\n\tread := 0\n\n\tfor m := range markers {\n\t\tif m.DoseRate <= 0 {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tcell, _ := h3.LatLngToCell(h3.NewLatLng(m.Lat, m.Lon), res)\n\t\tacc := cells[cell]\n\t\tif acc == nil {\n\t\t\tacc = &cellAcc{}\n\t\t\tcells[cell] = acc\n\t\t}\n\t\tacc.sumDose += m.DoseRate\n\t\tacc.count++\n\t\tif m.DoseRate > acc.maxDose {\n\t\t\tacc.maxDose = m.DoseRate\n\t\t}\n\t\tread++\n\t\tif read >= maxMarkers {\n\t\t\tcancelStream() // stop the DB query early\n\t\t\tbreak\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\t// Drain remainder so the goroutine can exit.\n\tfor range markers {}\n\n\t// Drain error channel — context cancellation after cap is expected and normal.\n\tif e := <-errCh; e != nil && streamCtx.Err() == nil && r.Context().Err() == nil {\n\t\twriteError(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, e.Error())\n\t\treturn\n\t}",
  "new_string": "\t// Use the H3-optimised streaming function: SQL LIMIT + TABLESAMPLE at low zoom\n\t// so Postgres stops early rather than scanning millions of rows.\n\tconst maxMarkers = 200_000\n\tmarkers, errCh := db.StreamMarkersForH3(r.Context(), 0, minLat, minLon, maxLat, maxLon, *dbType, maxMarkers)\n\n\ttype cellAcc struct {\n\t\tsumDose float64\n\t\tmaxDose float64\n\t\tcount   int\n\t}\n\tcells := make(map[h3.Cell]*cellAcc)\n\n\tfor m := range markers {\n\t\tif m.DoseRate <= 0 {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tcell, _ := h3.LatLngToCell(h3.NewLatLng(m.Lat, m.Lon), res)\n\t\tacc := cells[cell]\n\t\tif acc == nil {\n\t\t\tacc = &cellAcc{}\n\t\t\tcells[cell] = acc\n\t\t}\n\t\tacc.sumDose += m.DoseRate\n\t\tacc.count++\n\t\tif m.DoseRate > acc.maxDose {\n\t\t\tacc.maxDose = m.DoseRate\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t// Drain error channel — context cancellation is normal.\n\tif e := <-errCh; e != nil && r.Context().Err() == nil {\n\t\twriteError(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, e.Error())\n\t\treturn\n\t}"
}

> AGENT

Restart and test. The key improvement: at zoom ≤7, Postgres now samples only 5% of table pages (`TABLESAMPLE SYSTEM(5)`) plus a `LIMIT 200000` — so instead of scanning millions of rows, it reads a random 5% and stops. Should drop from 30s → 1-2s at low zoom. At zoom 9+ the sampling is 15% or 100% depending on area size, which keeps high-zoom views accurate.

1571-// =========

1573://  2. Pairwise speed: for each neighbour pair with Δt>0 compute v=d/Δt; this
1574-//     gives нам хотя бы несколько валидных скоростей.
1575-/  3. Gap-filling:  for любой непрерывной серии Speed==0
1576-//     ┌ both neighbours exist ─► take avg speed between them
--
1583-//
1584-// Complexity O(N), lock-free — the slice is owned by this goroutine.
1585-//
1586:// Limits: speeds outside 0…1000 m/s (≈0…3600 km/h) are considered glitches
1587-//
1588-//	and ignored while calculating new values.
1589-//
--
1594-//
1595-/ Why this change:

1597://     if treated as seconds, yields near-zero speeds. We detect ms and divide by 1000.

8902		// PERFORMANCE: Calculate density-based sampling rate for low zoom levels

8904		sampleRate := calculateSampleRate(zoom, minLat, minLon, maxLat, maxLon)
8905		if sampleRate < 1.0 {
8906			log.Printf("density reduction: zoom=%d rate=%.2f (sending ~%.0f%% of markers)",
8907				zoom, sampleRate, sampleRate*100)
8908		}
8909	
8910		// ON-THE-FLY CLUSTERING: Always query zoom=0 (raw markers)
8911		// The aggregateMarkers function will cluster based on requested zoom
8912		const rawZoom = 0
8913	
8914		// Choose streaming source: either entire map, a single track, or multiple tracks
8915		// When showFilter=="rt", skip historical data entirely for faster RT-only views.
8916		ctx := r.Context()
8917		var (
8918			baseSrc <-chan database.Marker
8919			errCh   <-chan error
8920		)
8921		rtOnly := showFilter == "rt"
8922		if rtOnly && trackID == "" && trackIDsParam == "" {
8923			// In RT-only mode for global view, still load historical markers
8924			// They will be filtered client-side, but spectrum markers should be available
8925			baseSrc, errCh = db.StreamMarkersByZoomAndBounds(ctx, rawZoom, minLat, minLon, maxLat, maxLon, *dbType)
8926		} else if trackID != "" {
8927			baseSrc, errCh = db.StreamMarkersByTrackIDZoomAndBounds(ctx, trackID, rawZoom, minLat, minLon, maxLat, maxLon, *dbType)
8928		} else if trackIDsParam != "" {
8929			// Handle multiple tracks by merging their streams
8930			trackIDs := strings.Split(trackIDsParam, ",")
8931			baseSrc, errCh = streamMultipleTracks(ctx, trackIDs, rawZoom, minLat, minLon, maxLat, maxLon, *dbType)
8932		} else {
8933			baseSrc, errCh = db.StreamMarkersByZoomAndBounds(ctx, rawZoom, minLat, minLon, maxLat, maxLon, *dbType)
8934		}
8935	
8936		// Fetch current realtime points once so the map reflects network devices.
8937		// We only touch the realtime table when the dedicated flag enables it so
8938		// operators control the feature explicitly.
8939		var rtMarks []database.Marker
8940		if *safecastRealtimeEnabled {
8941			var err error
8942			rtMarks, err = db.GetLatestRealtimeByBounds(ctx, minLat, minLon, maxLat, maxLon, *dbType)
8943			if err != nil {
8944				log.Printf("realtime query: %v", err)
8945			}
8946			// Log bounds alongside count to help diagnose empty map tiles.
8947			log.Printf("realtime markers: %d lat[%f,%f] lon[%f,%f]", len(rtMarks), minLat, maxLat, minLon, maxLon)
8948		}
8949	
8950		// Apply density-based sampling if needed (zoom < 8)
8951		var sampledSrc <-chan database.Marker
8952		if sampleRate < 1.0 {
8953			sampledSrc = sampleMarkerChannel(ctx, baseSrc, sampleRate)
8954		} else {
8955			sampledSrc = baseSrc
8956		}
8957	
8958		agg := aggregateMarkers(ctx, sampledSrc, nil, zoom)
8959	
8960		// Set appropriate headers based on format
8961		if useMsgpack {
8962			w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/octet-stream")
8963			w.Header().Set("X-Content-Format", "msgpack")
8964		} else {
8965			w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "text/event-stream")
8966		}

2893	// GetLatestRealtimeByBounds returns the newest reading per device within bounds.
2894	// We keep SQL portable and filter duplicates in Go, following "Clear is better than clever".
2895	func (db *Database) GetLatestRealtimeByBounds(ctx context.Context, minLat, minLon, maxLat, maxLon float64, dbType string) ([]Marker, error) {
2896		var query string
2897		switch strings.ToLower(dbType) {
2898		case "pgx", "duckdb":
2899			query = `
2900	SELECT id,device_id,transport,device_name,tube,country,value,unit,lat,lon,measured_at,extra
2901	FROM realtime_measurements
2902	WHERE lat BETWEEN $1 AND $2 AND lon BETWEEN $3 AND $4
2903	ORDER BY device_id,fetched_at DESC;`
2904		default:
2905			query = `
2906	SELECT id,device_id,transport,device_name,tube,country,value,unit,lat,lon,measured_at,extra
2907	FROM realtime_measurements
2908	WHERE lat BETWEEN ? AND ? AND lon BETWEEN ? AND ?
2909	ORDER BY device_id,fetched_at DESC;`
2910		}
2911	
2912		var out []Marker
2913		if ctx == nil {
2914			ctx = context.Background()
2915		}
2916		ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, serializedWaitFloor)
2917		defer cancel()
2918	
2919		err := db.withSerializedConnectionFor(ctx, WorkloadWebRead, func(jobCtx context.Context, conn *sql.DB) error {
2920			rows, err := conn.QueryContext(jobCtx, query, minLat, maxLat, minLon, maxLon)
2921			if err != nil {
2922				return fmt.Errorf("query realtime: %w", err)
2923			}
2924			defer rows.Close()
2925	
2926			now := time.Now().Unix()
2927			const daySeconds = int64((24 * time.Hour) / time.Second)
2928	
2929			seen := make(map[string]bool)
2930			for rows.Next() {
2931				var (
2932					rowID                                        int64
2933					id, transport, name, tube, country, extraRaw string
2934					val                                          float64
2935					unit                                         string
2936					lat, lon                                     float64
2937					measured                                     int64
2938				)
2939				if err := rows.Scan(&rowID, &id, &transport, &name, &tube, &country, &val, &unit, &lat, &lon, &measured, &extraRaw); err != nil {
2940					return fmt.Errorf("scan realtime: %w", err)
2941				}
2942				if lat == 0 && lon == 0 {
2943					continue // skip bogus locations at the equator
2944				}
2945				if val <= 0 {
2946					continue // ignore non-positive readings
2947				}
2948				if now-measured > daySeconds {
2949					continue // drop devices that have been silent for more than a day
2950				}
2951				if seen[id] {
2952					continue // keep the newest reading only once per device
2953				}
2954	
2955				var (
2956					doseRate float64
2957					ok       bool
2958				)
2959				if realtimeConverter != nil {
2960					doseRate, ok = realtimeConverter(val, unit)
2961				}
2962				if !ok {
2963					continue
2964				}
2965	
2966				var extras map[string]float64
2967				trimmed := strings.TrimSpace(extraRaw)
2968				if trimmed != "" {
2969					if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(trimmed), &extras); err != nil {
2970						log.Printf("parse realtime extra for %s: %v", id, err)
2971					}
2972				}

8968		flusher, ok := w.(http.Flusher)
8969		if !ok {
8970			http.Error(w, "Streaming unsupported", http.StatusInternalServerError)
8971			return
8972		}
8973	
8974		// Helper to write a msgpack frame: 4-byte length prefix + data
8975		writeMsgpackFrame := func(data []byte) {
8976			lenBuf := make([]byte, 4)
8977			binary.BigEndian.PutUint32(lenBuf, uint32(len(data)))
8978			w.Write(lenBuf)
8979			w.Write(data)
8980		}
8981	
8982		// Emit realtime markers first when enabled.

8984			if useMsgpack {
8985				b, _ := msgpack.Marshal(m)
8986				writeMsgpackFrame(b)
8987			} else {
8988				b, _ := json.Marshal(m)
8989				fmt.Fprintf(w, "data: %s\n\n", b)
8990			}
8991		}
8992		flusher.Flush()
8993	
8994		for {
8995			select {
8996			case <-ctx.Done():
8997				return
8998			case err, ok := <-errCh:
8999				if !ok {
9000					errCh = nil
9001					continue
9002				}
9003				if err != nil {
9004					if useMsgpack {
9005						// Send zero-length frame as error indicator
9006						w.Write([]byte{0, 0, 0, 0})
9007					} else {
9008						fmt.Fprintf(w, "event: done\ndata: %v\n\n", err)
9009					}
9010					flusher.Flush()
9011					return
9012				}
9013				errCh = nil
9014			case m, ok := <-agg:
9015				if !ok {
9016					if useMsgpack {
9017						// Send zero-length frame as end marker
9018						w.Write([]byte{0, 0, 0, 0})
9019					} else {
9020						fmt.Fprint(w, "event: done\ndata: end\n\n")
9021					}
9022					flusher.Flush()
9023					return
9024				}
9025				if useMsgpack {
9026					b, _ := msgpack.Marshal(m)
9027					writeMsgpackFrame(b)
9028				} else {
9029					b, _ := json.Marshal(m)
9030					fmt.Fprintf(w, "data: %s\n\n", b)
9031				}
9032				flusher.Flush()
9033			}
9034		}

{
  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "// zoomToH3Resolution maps a Leaflet zoom level to a sensible H3 resolution.\n// Target: 200–2000 visible hex cells per viewport.\nfunc zoomToH3Resolution(zoom int) int {\n\t// One resolution finer than before — hexagons are ~3–5x smaller per step.\n\t// res 2 = ~86km edge, res 3 = ~33km, res 4 = ~12km, res 5 = ~4.5km,\n\t// res 6 = ~1.7km, res 7 = ~0.6km, res 8 = ~0.2km\n\tswitch {\n\tcase zoom <= 3:\n\t\treturn 2\n\tcase zoom <= 5:\n\t\treturn 3\n\tcase zoom <= 7:\n\t\treturn 4\n\tcase zoom <= 9:\n\t\treturn 5\n\tcase zoom <= 10:\n\t\treturn 6\n\tcase zoom <= 11:\n\t\treturn 7\n\tdefault:\n\t\treturn 8\n\t}\n}",
  "new_string": "// zoomToH3Resolution maps a Leaflet zoom level to a sensible H3 resolution.\n// Target: 200–2000 visible hex cells per viewport.\nfunc zoomToH3Resolution(zoom int) int {\n\t// res 3 = ~33km edge, res 4 = ~12km, res 5 = ~4.5km,\n\t// res 6 = ~1.7km, res 7 = ~0.6km, res 8 = ~0.2km, res 9 = ~70m\n\tswitch {\n\tcase zoom <= 3:\n\t\treturn 3\n\tcase zoom <= 5:\n\t\treturn 4\n\tcase zoom <= 7:\n\t\treturn 5\n\tcase zoom <= 9:\n\t\treturn 6\n\tcase zoom <= 11:\n\t\treturn 7\n\tcase zoom <= 13:\n\t\treturn 8\n\tdefault:\n\t\treturn 9\n\t}\n}"
}
